Remarks.&mdash;During April the highest reading of the barometer was 30.266, on the 9th; and the lowest 29.816, on the 4th. The highest temperature was 90&deg;, on the 30th; the lowest 62&deg;, on the 1st; and the mean during month was 740.9. s.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 4th, averaging 8.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 11 days, measuring 4.6 inches. During May the highest reading of the barometer was 30.286, on the 4th; and the lowest 29.798, on the 27th. The highest temperature was 930, on the 17th; the lowest 67&deg;, on the 19th; and the mean during month was 79&deg;.9. s.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 2nd, averaging 10.5 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 19 days, measuring 6.72 inches. During June the highest reading of the barometer was 30.100, on the 9th; and the lowest 29.700, on the 25th. The highest temperature was 98&deg;, on the 26th; the lowest 720, on the 3rd and 4th; and the mean during month was 84&deg;.2. s.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 12th, averaging 14.9 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 12 days, measuring 3.68 inches. IX. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 80, 1896.
NOTICES- TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 292. KIUNGCHOW DISTRICT. BUOYAGE OF HAINAN STRAIT. NOTicE is hereby given that tke Riversdale le, which lies to the south-eastward of Hainan:Head, with Single Palm bearing S. 69jo W., magnetic, distant about 18j( cables, and which has a depth Of 31 fathoms on it at low water of spring tides, is now marked by a bkwlk conical buoy, surmounted by a black triangular cage. The buoy is moored in 9) fathoms, about ij -cables to the north-eastward of the rock, and must be left on the port hAnd by vessels entering the Strait from the eastward. The Magpie Rock Buoy has been removed. DIRECTIONS. With the Riversdale Rock Buoy in the position above described, and the Hainan Head Bank Buoy and Hainan Reef Buoy in their advertised positions, vessels using the South Channel should steer to pass the two former buoys at about 3 cables, taking care to guard against being set towards the shore, and remembering that it is safe to borrow towards the banks on the starboard hand-of which the lead, which should be kept goiug, will give warning,-while on the other side the bottom is robky and uneven and the lead gives no warning. The Hainan Reef Buoy may be rounded at a distance of i cable or more at discretion. SHANRU, ist Api 1895.
.CHIFOO 01STRICT. NoTicE is hereby given that Captain JoN C. BRoADiURST, of the Nicaraguan barque Comet, reports that his vessel, drawing 17 feet, struck on a pinnacle rock, the approdmate position of which is- South-east Shantung Promontory S.W. by W. j W., magnetic, Lighthouse . . nit.. . Ears Rock . . . . . W. j S., magnetic, where the Admiralty charts show 23 fathoms. The accident occurred about 31 hours before high water on a neap tide. SHANGHAI, 17th April 1895. No. 294. HIESHAN ISLANDS-NINGPO DISTRICT. PEI-YUY-SHAN LIGHTHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given that the light on Pei-yu-shan was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 28th instant. The illuminating apparatus is revolving dioptric, hyper-radial, showing double twhite flashes at intervals of half a minute, thus:- Flash .. ......... ....... . i seconds. Eclipse .... 5 Flash ......... ......... . . ,,.. Eclipse... ............ ..22 -The light-tower stands a little to the north-westward of the face of the remarkable cliff which forms the south-eastern extreme of Pei-yti-shan-marked on the British Admiralty Chat No. 1,759 as Shaho Island,-and the light, which is elevated 345 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 26 nautical miles in all directions where it is not obscured by outlying islands.
The obscuration caused by Nan-ydi-shan (Saddle Island) is, approximately, between the rings N. 42&#39; 16&#39; E. gad N. 55 16&#39; E., but near its,extreme range the light will show between two peaks of Nan-yt-shan when it bears between N. 46&#39; 13&#39;E. and N. 5o* 3&#39;E. The bearings are magnetic and from seaward.. The tower is round, of iron, 25 feet high, with a total height from base to the lantern vae of S 51 feet. The tower, dwellings, and boundary wall are painted white. Approximate position- Latitude . . . . . . . . . . . 280 53&#39; 15&quot; N. Longitude . . . . .....5&#39; 4o E. SHANGoAI, 30th MaY 1895. f.2

(BeK&quot;se Isth JWdY 1895*) KOWLOON, STR JuLr i89.5 I.-&amp;amp; In. conformity with the instructions contained in your Cironiaz0No. 38t, Second Series, I now transmit, to the Statiscal Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statisties of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring statiqns in Natie vessels for the second quarter of the year 189S9 April-Jane. 6. The Oustom House ( i3) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collecton of Provincial Liki? a Ching-tei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium ( 2 0 3o and Hkk Bo per pioul) commenced on the 14th April. 887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the iAe fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present ontrolled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capuimoon( Changehow (F ), Potochow gg ),and Kowloon ( )stations c. The word &quot;Inwards1 in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&#39;from China. II.
